## Document 056: 2026年9月美国CPI数据点评：美国9月CPI：风险在油价的Higher for Longer

宏观点评 20261015  
美国 9 月 CPI：风险在 油价的 Higher for 
Longer——2026 年 9 月美国 CPI 数据点评

2026 年 10 月 15 日

相关研究  
《A 股 9 月复盘与 10 月展望——缩
量调整中的结构性分化，与政策托底
的十字路口》 
2026-10-14 
《 9 月美国 CPI 前瞻： 核心环比预计
温和，仍需关注油价扰动 ——海外周
报 20261011》 
2026-10-11

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 
◼ 核心观点：9 月美国 CPI 全面超预期，核心项目是超预期的主要来源。
与 8 月少数项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与服务环比的反弹具有广泛
性，通胀扩散度显著走高，超级核心通胀 3 个月均年率升至 4%以上的
高位，且二手车、 房租等分项近期走势与领先指数 “脱钩” 。这一通胀黏
性背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性超预期，充分条件则是油价
“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的传导。展望看，若油价持续在
高位反复， 则高油价给明年通胀带来的基数效应利好的意义将减弱。货
币政策方面，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否对 11 月 3 日的美国中期选举
尤其重要， 因此虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高， 但我们更担忧伊朗在 10
月下旬升级冲突、给特朗普施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息
预期走高的风险。就策略而言，中期选举仍是一个重要的事件节点，中
选后各方暂时消停、 美国国会格局清晰，市场的不确定性才会消除；我
们维持此前观点：10 月的不确定性高， 对风险偏好构成持续压制， 更适
合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 美国 9 月 CPI：全面超预期， 加息预期升温。美国 9 月 CPI 环比+0.65%，
预期+0.6%， 前值+0.4%； 核心CPI 环比+0.33%， 预期+0.2%， 前值+0.29%。
CPI 同比+3.74%，预期+3.6%，前值+3.40%；核心 CPI 同比+2.56%，预
期+2.5%，前值+2.45%。由于总体 CPI 环比基本符合预期，核心项目的
超预期是 9 月通胀全面超预期的主要驱动，且核心 CPI 环比是在 8 月
“异常”的高基数上延续高于预期，带动核心 CPI 环比 3 个月均值显
著走高。 数据发布后，紧货币交易强化，交易员加息定价10 月 28 日美
联储加息概率从 20%左右升至最高 50%以上，全年加息预期最高升至约
1.5 次，美股和黄金下跌，美债收益率和美元上涨。随后，数位美联储
官员发言仍释放鸽派信号，表示不急于加息，10 月加息预期回落至约
30%，美债利率回落。 
◼ 通胀结构：核心分项环比全面走高，通胀广度扩散。总体来看，核心商
品、 居住服务、 除居住核心服务等核心CPI 大类在 9 月均环比反弹， 显
示通胀黏性仍大。①核心商品方面，9 月核心商品 CPI 环比+0.25%，为
2025 年 2 月以来最高， 前值+0.11%；结构上， 全球供应链压力、 关税和
AI 相关商品涨价均是贡献来源。其中，高占比的二手车价格拉动 9.6bps，
是核心商品环比上涨的主要贡献，且与其领先指标 Manheim 二手车走
势脱钩。与关税相关的休闲商品与服装， 二者环比分别由上月的+0.02%、
0%反弹至+0.31%、+0.86%，其中休闲商品中的玩具环比涨幅突出，拉
动核心 CPI 环比 6.6bps。②居住通胀方面，9 月居住通胀环比+0.39%，
较前值 +0.26%显著 反弹， 其 中租 金和 自住 房折算 环 比分别 反弹 至
+0.25%、+0.35%的较高中枢， 酒店住宿环比保持在+2.04%的高位。从领
先指标——领先约 16 个月的房价和领先约 6—8 个月的租金来看，居住
通胀应当仍有一定的下行空间，其在本月的反弹略超预期。③超级核心
通胀方面，由于上月的电信服务等一次性扰动的消退，9 月非居住核心
服务通胀环比由前值+0.42%回落至+0.34%， 超级核心通胀 （包括居住服
务中的酒店住宿） 环比由前值+0.51%回落至+0.42%，但环比均处于较高
中枢，且 3 个月均年率分别升至 4.43%、4.48%的高位。尤其是，从结
构来看，交运、休闲服务、酒店等更广泛的项目出现了上涨，超级核心
通胀结构所体现的通胀黏性更加突出。④通胀扩散度方面，反映通胀广
度的通胀扩散指数在 9 月显著走高，环比折年增速大于过去 5 年均值的
项目占比从 17.6%提升至 24.76%，环比折年增速大于 4%的项目占比从
20%提升至 35%。 
◼ 展望与策略启示： 风险在于油价的“higher for longer”：总体来看，9 月
美国 CPI 全面超预期， 其中核心项目是超预期的主要来源。 与8 月少数
项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与核心服务环比的反弹具有广泛性，通
胀扩散度显著走高， 我们认为其背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性

### Page 2

2 / 7 
超预期，充分条件则是油价“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的
传导， 这对美联储来说是一个不容乐观的信号。展望看，①通胀趋势方
面，若油价持续在高位反复，则我们此前看好的“高油价带来的基数效
应”意义减弱：一来是油价的回落幅度有限， 产生的基数效应更加有限；
二来是高油价的持续意味着通胀更大的扩散风险。②货币政策方面，对
于特朗普而言，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否，对 11 月 3 日的美国中期
选举尤其重要，因此，虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高，但我们更担忧上
行风险，即伊朗在中期选举前的 10 月下旬主动挑高油价、给特朗普政
府施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息预期走高的风险。就策略
启示而言， 我们强调在此前报告中的观点， 美国中期选举仍然是一个重
要的事件节点，中选后美伊双方暂时消停、美国国会格局清晰， 市场的
不确定性才会逐步消除；10 月的不确定性高，对风险偏好构成持续压
制，因此更适合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 风险提示：油价失控推高通胀； 美联储维持高利率水平时间过长， 引发
金融系统流动性危机。

免责及评级说明部分

东吴证券投资评级标准 
投资评级基于分析师对报告发布日后 6 至 12 个月内行业或公司回报潜力相对基准表现
的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普 
500 指数， 新三板基准指数为三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转
让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50 指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。 
我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。 我们采用的是相
对评级体系， 表示投资的相对比重建议。 投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定应当充分考虑自身
特定状况， 如具体投资目的、 财务状况以及特定需求等， 并完整理解和使用本报告内容， 不
应视本报告为做出投资决策的唯一因素。

## Document 057: 2026年9月贸易数据点评：进出口双双超预期，出口增势多点发散

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月贸易数据点评：进出口双双超预期，出口增势多点发散.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
ooo[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 10 月 15 日      
证 券研究报告 •宏观简评报告      
数据点评  
进出口双双超预期，出口增势多点发散  
——2026 年 9 月贸易数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：从贸易总体情况看，9 月我国货物贸易出口、 进口同比分别增长36.3%、
31.0%，较上月分别加快 11.3、2.8 个百分点；1-9 月进出口总额同比增长
23.8%，贸易顺差达 9381.1亿美元。展望后续，全球AI 算力投资、汽车及机
电产品出口有望继续提供支撑， 但高基数、海外需求波动及贸易政策不确定性
可能带来扰动。从贸易伙伴看，9 月我国对东盟、韩国、欧盟出口同比分别增
长 52.3%、68.2%、25.3%，较上月分别加快22.1、18.9、18.6个百分点，反
映区域产业链贸易联系深化及 AI 产业链需求增长；对欧出口提速也可能受到
企业提前出货影响。对美贸易增速同步回升， 中美关税减让共识有望改善双边
贸易预期，但具体安排仍待落地。从出口结构看，机电产品、高新技术产品出
口金额同比分别增长43.7%、63.6%，集成电路、自动数据处理设备及零部件
出口分别增长 136.0%、92.9%， 全球AI 算力基础设施建设持续提供需求支撑。
不过，集成电路出口数量仅增长 1.1%，出口金额高增仍较多受到价格因素推
动。 汽车、 家电出口金额分别增长62.0%、31.7%， 出口数量分别增长55.5%、
25.4%，量价共同支撑出口增长；成品油出口金额增长 125.1%，出口动能进
一步扩散。 从进口结构看，集成电路、 自动数据处理设备及零部件进口金额分
别增长 74.7%、296.5%，机床进口金额增长 23.8%，电子产业链和高端制造
相关需求保持较强。 原油进口金额同比增长3.3%， 但进口数量仍下降21.5%，
金额回升主要受到国际油价上涨支撑；铁矿石进口金额、 数量分别下降7.4%、
5.6%，反映传统资源品进口需求偏弱。汽车进口金额和数量分别下降38.1%、
47.2%，国产车型替代效应延续。整体看，9 月外贸增长动能有所扩散，出口
受 AI 产业链、汽车及机电产品共同支撑，进口则呈现高技术产品偏强、部分
资源品及汽车偏弱的结构性分化。 
 外贸表现超预期，进出口双双提速。按美元计，2026 年 1‑ 9 月，我国货物贸
易进出口总额同比增长23.8%， 涨幅相比1‑ 8 月扩大 1.4 个百分点， 持续保持
高位。1‑ 9 月，贸易顺差为 9381.12亿美元，相比 1‑8 月增加 1326.07 亿美
元。按美元计，9 月单月， 货物贸易出口、 进口同比分别增长36.3%、31.0%，
涨幅分别较 8 月回升 11.3 和 2.8 个百分点。9 月出口增长主要受 AI产业链需
求旺盛、 汽车及机电产品出口走强支撑，同时区域产业链贸易联系深化， 对欧
出口也可能存在提前出货现象。进口方面，电子链产品进口保持强势，部分资
源品进口表现分化， 原油进口金额回升则受到国际油价上涨支撑。从景气表现
来看，9 月美欧日制造业景气保持扩张： 标普全球美国制造业PMI 终值为 55.9，
创下自 2022 年 5 月以来的最高水平，产出和新订单以更快的速度扩张；欧元
区制造业 PMI终值上升至 52.9，新订单以自2022年 3月以来最快的速度增长，
出口销售连续第二个月扩大；日本制造业 PMI 终值回落至 54.1，企业记录到
产出和新订单的增长放缓。贸易政策方面，9 月下旬中美就 300 亿美元对等降
税达成框架共识， 但安排尚未落地；降税范围集中于消费品等非敏感品类，高
端制造领域贸易壁垒保持不变。10 月中欧开展贸易磋商，双方就混动汽车达
成贸易谅解，并继续推进电动车反补贴案价格承诺后续程序，有助于降低短期
贸易冲突升级风险。10 月以来，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡的紧张局势持续升温，
伊朗加强对海上运输活动限制， 美国持续强化对伊朗石油贸易的封锁，区域供

### Page 2

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
2
应风险上升。整体看，9月进出口同步提速，出口增长动能相对多元，进口则
更多受到电子产业链需求及大宗商品价格变化影响。预计AI 算力投资、汽车
及机电产品出口将继续提供支撑， 但高基数、 海外需求波动及贸易政策不确定
性可能带来扰动， 部分企业提前出货也可能透支后续需求。进口方面，电子产
业链需求有望延续支撑， 能源及资源品进口则更多取决于国内生产需求和国际
价格变化。
 出口高增覆盖多元贸易伙伴，“抢出口”效应带动对欧出口走强。以美元计，
从贸易总额看，9 月我国与主要贸易伙伴的进出口均有所改善。 对东盟、 欧盟、
美国的进出口总额同比增速分别扩大 13.8、12.0、4.8 个百分点至 45.8%、
16.8%、34.8%。其中，对东盟贸易增速提升幅度较大，反映区域产业链贸易
联系持续深化；对美贸易方面，9 月我国对美进出口总额同比增速有所回升。
中美达成 300 亿美元进口商品关税减让共识， 有望改善后续双边贸易预期， 后
续仍需关注具体降税安排及订单变化。从出口总额看，9 月我国对主要贸易伙

伴出口普遍提速。对东盟、韩国、欧盟的出口同比增速分别扩大22.1、18.9、
18.6 个百分点至 52.3%、68.2%、25.3%。东盟和韩国出口增速显著提升，结
合同期集成电路等电子产品出口高增的表现，全球AI 算力投资和半导体产业
链需求可能是重要支撑。对欧出口增速明显回升，可能受到部分企业提前安排
出货的影响。考虑到欧盟持续讨论限制中国汽车进口，相关贸易政策不确定性
可能促使车企提前交付，带动短期对欧出口增长。从进口总额看，9 月我国对
主要贸易伙伴的进口增速整体变化不大，值得注意的是，对韩国进口同比增速
收窄 13.8 个百分点至 94.3%，但仍处于较高水平。结合韩国半导体出口持续
高增的表现，全球 AI 算力投资带来的芯片需求仍是重要支撑。对韩进口增速
回落可能受到商品结构、价格及基数等因素影响。
 出口增长动能多点扩散，高技术出口景气延续。以美元计，从出口金额看，集
成电路、成品油、自动数据处理设备及零部件、汽车（包括底盘）出口金额同
比分别增长 136.0%、125.1%、92.9%和 62.0%；陶瓷产品、肥料、粮食出口

金额同比分别下降 19.1%、9.2%和 5.3%，其中陶瓷和肥料降幅均有所收窄。
从出口数量看，汽车（包括底盘）、成品油、未锻轧铝及铝材、家用电器出口
数量同比分别增长 55.5%、39.3%、26.1%和 25.4%；此前同比下降的稀土、
肥料、船舶出口数量均转为增长，手机、粮食出口数量则分别下降 12.6%和
7.1%。从出口结构看，9 月机电产品、高新技术产品出口金额同比分别增长
43.7%和 63.6%，占出口总额的比重分别为66.7%和 32.9%，仍是出口增长的
重要支撑。其中，集成电路出口金额同比增长136.0%，出口数量同比仅增长
1.1%， 但已由上月下降转为增长， 出口金额高增仍主要受到价格上涨推动； 自
动数据处理设备及零部件出口金额同比增长92.9%，全球 AI 算力基础设施建
设带来的需求增长仍是重要支撑。从边际变化看， 资源品出口表现分化。 肥料
出口金额同比下降 9.2%，降幅收窄 52.7 个百分点，出口数量同比转为增长
12.7%；成品油出口金额同比增长 125.1%，出口数量同比增长 39.3%，数量

和金额同比涨幅均扩大。劳动密集型产品方面，箱包、鞋靴、陶瓷等产品出口
金额和数量同比表现均有所改善， 但陶瓷出口金额仍下降19.1%。机电产品方
面， 手机和船舶出口金额同比增速分别收窄15.4 和 18.3 个百分点至 14.2%和
2.7%， 但两者出口数量增速均较上月改善。 相比之下， 汽车和家电出口金额同
比增速分别扩大至 62.0%和 31.7%， 出口数量同比增速也分别扩大至55.5%和
25.4%，呈现量价共同支撑的特征。总体而言，9月出口增长由 AI产业链、汽
车、 家电及成品油等多个品类共同支撑， 其中芯片出口金额增长对价格变化较
为敏感，汽车和家电则有较强的实物量支撑。后续来看，外需韧性及 AI 算力
基础设施建设有望继续支撑集成电路、 计算机设备等产品出口，但高基数及海
外需求波动可能对增速形成扰动。
 能源及资源品进口量承压，AI链产品量价现改善。以美元计， 从农产品及能源
进口看，粮食和大豆进口金额同比分别增长6.0%和 2.8%，增速均边际回落；
进口数量同比分别下降2.2%和 7.7%，主要受高基数叠加国内豆粕饲用需求偏

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
弱影响。原油进口金额同比由降转升至3.3%，进口数量同比下降 21.5%，降
幅继续收窄。金额与数量走势分化，主要受到国际油价上涨影响， 原油进口实
物量仍处于深度收缩区间。从资源品进口看，9 月铁矿砂及其精矿进口金额、
数量同比分别下降 7.4%和 5.6%，均由升转降，反映黑色产业链相关进口需求
有所走弱。肥料进口金额、数量同比分别增长22.6%和 23.4%，涨幅均较上月
收窄 50 多个百分点，主要是前期进口集中释放之后的正常回调。钢材进口金
额同比增长 4.6%，数量同比下降 10.6%，降幅有所收窄，实物进口量仍然偏
弱。从高技术及机电产品进口看，集成电路进口金额同比增长74.7%，增速收
窄 8.9 个百分点，而进口数量同比仅增长1.7%，金额与数量走势明显分化，
芯片价格上涨及进口产品结构变化是金额高增长的重要原因。 自动数据处理设
备及其零部件进口金额同比增速扩大87.5 个百分点至 296.5%， 反映相关设备
进口金额显著走强。机床进口金额同比由降转升至 23.8%，数量同比增长

7.7%，反映高端制造需求扩张的趋势持续。 汽车进口金额、 数量同比分别下降
38.1%和 47.2%，其中金额降幅扩大、数量降幅收窄，整体进口仍处于明显收
缩区间，反映国产高端新能源车型的替代效应持续显现。整体来看，9 月进口
延续结构性分化， 电子设备及高端制造相关产品进口走强， 能源及部分资源品
进口偏弱，汽车进口持续收缩。展望后续，高技术及高端制造相关产品有望成
为进口增长的重要支撑， 能源及资源品进口仍取决于国内需求与国际价格， 汽
车进口则继续面临国产车型的替代压力。
 风险提示：国内政策落地不及预期，海外经济波动超预期， 地缘政治风险超预
期。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌 幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

Hon Hai (2317.TW): Sep beat: revenues +38% YoY; New AI servers, CPO 
switch and Foldable phones to drive 4Q26; Buy (on CL)
5 October 2026 | 11:23PM HKT
Hon Hai’s Sep revenues were +38% YoY/ 26% MoM to NT$1.2tn, or 15% / 21% 
higher than our / Bloomberg consensus estimates, reflecting strong AI servers ramp 
up and new smartphone models in contribution. From a MoM perspective, 
Consumer electronics is the strongest, followed by Components, Cloud and 
Networking and Computing, and all segments are positive MoM. The strong MoM 
reaffirms our positive view on the company’s leading market position across: (1) 
Smartphone: serving global leading brands, which suffer less under high memory 
costs, with new model launches and form factor changes (report link), driving the 
shipment and ASP increase, (2) AI servers: serving global leading CSP customers, 
and early in the delivery of the latest AI server racks, driving the shipment and ASP 
increase. The company is also the major supplier of CPO switch, supporting Sep and 
4Q26 growth. From a YoY perspective, Cloud and Networking is the strongest, 
followed by Components, Computing, and Consumer electronics, and all segments 
are positive YoY. We attribute the slower YoY in consumer electronics to its major 
brand customers’ fewer model launches this year, along with the new form factor 
(foldable phones) being not available in Sep, and a modest smartphone market

Valuation: Our 12m TP for Hon Hai is unchanged at NT$400 and remains based on a 
P/E. Our target 2027E P/E multiple of 17.9x (unchanged) is derived from a peers’ 
average ratio of trading P/E to forward year NI YoY growth and OPM (P/E divided by the 
sum of NI YoY and OPM). Our target P/E of 17.9x is at the high-end of the company’s 
historical forward P/E, reflecting our positive view on Hon Hai’s business expansion from 
the competitive consumer electronics market to AI servers and EVs outsourcing, which 
should drive profit expansion with diversifying business opportunities for Hon Hai. 
Maintain Buy (on CL).

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM).

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

在完成客戶 5,000 小時可靠度測試認證後，光環代工業務自 26H2
起重新貢獻營運， 隨稼動率提升及產品組合優化， 本業獲利已顯著
改善並順利轉虧為盈 。 為配合客戶後續產能規劃， 公司亦將斥資約
11 億元進行產線擴充，預期至2027 年底產能規模將達 2026 年底
的 3 倍。此外，針對具 Scale-up 應用潛力的 VCSEL 技術，光環
亦規劃推出 1060nm VCSEL 方案供客戶驗證，有望切入新一代
AI 光互連市場，進一步強化公司中長期競爭優勢。綜合以上，研
究部預估光環 2026 年 EPS 0.57 元，2027 年 EPS 6.77 元，目前
PE 為 26.5x，參考光通上游同業 PE 評價為 20-60x，考量光環本
業獲利將開始由虧轉盈且顯著成長，且也開始佈局 Scale-up 雷射
方案，因此給予 35xPER，目標價 240 元，建議買進。 
⚫ 公司基本資料 ⚫ 公司基本資料 
光環(3234)成立於 1997 年，主要產品為光通訊主動元件，包含雷
射晶粒切割、次模組封裝，2025 年晶粒及零組件佔 63%、光電轉
換件 28%、其他 9%。應用領域則為頻寬網路 10%、4G/5G 基地
台互聯 31%、雲端數據中心 22%、感測應用 26%、其他 11%，銷
售地區為台灣 60%、 中國8%、 歐洲及美洲17%、 亞洲及其他15%。

殖利率(%) - - ⚫ 營運概況 
取得客戶測試認證，代工業務重回貢獻 
光環 26Q2 營收 2.0 億元，Q+7.5%，Y+28.1%，毛利率 19.82%，
稅後淨利-1744 萬元，Q 持續虧損，Y 持續虧損，EPS -0.16 元。
在重新取得客戶 5000 小時的測試認證下，代工收入逐步重回貢獻
且產品組合優化下，使整體毛利率回升至雙位數以上，並在費用控
管得宜讓本業獲利虧損顯著收斂。

代工事業加速成長，本業由虧轉盈 
光環 8 月營收 1.1 億元，M+13.1%，Y+110.1%，7 月營收 9604
萬元，M+25.2%，Y+96.9%，7-8 月合計 2.0 億元，Y+103.7%。
代工事業逐月增加片數 ， 研究部認為在稼動率回升及產品組合優化
下，光環獲利將持續獲得顯著改善，其中7 月自結稅後淨利約 900
萬，EPS 0.08 元，反映出本業隨營收的放大而已轉虧為盈。綜合
以 上 ， 研 究 部 預 估 光 環26Q3 營收 3.2 億元， Q+63.9% ，
Y+113.2%，毛利率 33.77%，稅後淨利 4462 萬元，Q 虧轉盈，Y
虧轉盈，EPS 0.35 元。

產能積極布局，同時開發 1060nm VCSEL 自有產品 
光環今年以來已完成 16.5 億元現金增資(稀釋 EPS 約 11.8%)，並
公告將斥資約 11 億元進行產能擴充及設備擴置。研究部推估，此
次擴產主要係配合客戶 2027～2028 年產能規劃 ， 因而提前建置相
對應代工設備，預期至 2027 年底整體產能規模有望達 2026 年底
的 3 倍，且公司亦已著手規劃 2028 年後續擴產計畫。綜上所述，
預期光環代工業務將自 26H2 起加速成長，隨產品組合改善，本業
可望轉為穩定獲利，加以產能利用率提升帶動營運槓桿逐步顯現，
預期整體獲利增幅將優於營收成長。此外，光環深耕VCSEL 市場
多 年 ， 而 今 年 以 來 多 家 國 際 光 通 訊 及 雷 射 大 廠 亦 陸 續 提 出 以

VCSEL 作為 Scale-up 光互連光源的方案 。 相較SiPh 方案 ，VCSEL
具備高頻寬密度、低功耗、低延遲潛力及高可靠度等優勢，過往傳
輸 距 離 較 短 的 限 制 ， 在 以 機 櫃 內 及 機 櫃 間 短 距 離 互 連 為 主 的
Scale-up 應用中影響亦相對有限。研究部認為，若未來 Scale-up
架構朝 Wide-and-Slow 方向發展，VCSEL 將成為極具競爭力的技
術選項。光環憑藉既有技術與量產經驗，目前也開始佈局1060nm
的 VCSEL 方案，預計於年底推出樣品給客戶測試，未來有望成為
強化公司長期競爭優勢的自有產品。綜合以上，研究部預估光環
2026 年營收 11.0 億元 ，Y+60.7%， 毛利率28.29%， 稅後淨利7233
萬元 ，Y 虧轉盈 ，EPS 0.57 元；2027 年營收 28.8 億元 ，Y+162.0%，
毛利率 40.89%，稅後淨利 8.6 億元，Y+1083.9%，EPS 6.77 元。

圖二：Scale-up 互連技術比較

資料來源：Lumentum、第一金投顧彙整

結論 
在完成客戶 5,000 小時可靠度測試認證後，光環代工業務自 26H2
起重新貢獻營運，隨稼動率提升及產品組合優化，本業獲利已顯著
改善並順利轉虧為盈。為配合客戶後續產能規劃，公司亦將斥資約
11 億元進行產線擴充， 預期至2027 年底產能規模將達 2026 年底
的 3 倍。此外，針對具 Scale-up 應用潛力的 VCSEL 技術，光環
亦規劃推出 1060nm VCSEL 方案供客戶驗證 ， 有望切入新一代AI
光互連市場，進一步強化公司中長期競爭優勢。綜合以上，研究部

## Document 114: 9月通胀点评：四季度通胀增速或走平

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月通胀点评：四季度通胀增速或走平.pdf
Report source: 中银证券
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济 | 证券研究报告 — 总量点评 2026 年 10 月 14 日

xiaojiao.zhang@bocichina.com 
证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300514010002

证券分析师：朱启兵 
(8610)66229359 
Qibing.Zhu@bocichina.com 
证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

9 月通胀点评

四季度通胀增速或走平   
9 月国际大宗商品价格波动对国内通胀的影响依然较大，国际油价、有色金
属价格造成明显上拉， 黄金价格则造成一定拖累；9 月 AI 产品价格上升对通
胀的影响进一步凸显，CPI 中通信工具分项同比增速连续两个月在 10%上方；
我们认为四季度 CPI 和 PPI 同比增速或在当前增速水平保持窄幅波动， 关注
国际局势、厄尔尼诺、生产端年底补库存等因素对通胀的扰动 。 
 9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，同比增长 0.9%，核心 CPI 同比增长 1.0%，服务
价格同比增长 0.8%，消费品价格同比增长 1.0%。从分项同比看，同比增
速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持
平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服务增速均
较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
 从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上月相同，低于季节性
水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点，服务价格由上
月上涨 0.1%转为下降 0.2%，对 CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百
分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 工业消费品价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点。 
 AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长
0.9%，其中，食品价格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点，汽油价格影
响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点，扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ；
从细项看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛
肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点，黄金饰品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百
分点，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致
的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能源价格和黄金价格的持续扰
动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面，9 月也出现了新的特
点，最为明显的是 AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8
月上升的重要因素 ， 体现在通信工具价格9 月同比增长 14.3%、 交通通信
项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代表的食品价格，出现
环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持
相对平稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天
气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，
以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。 
 9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 5.0%，其中生产资料同比增长 6.5%，
生活资料同比下降 0.1%。PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9% 
 关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于
万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业
价格，从行业看主要的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化工等受输入性因
素影响较大的行业，二是受 AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业，三是受
季节性因素影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不
确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具体情况 ， 都可能对PPI 价格造成影
响 ， 但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化 ， 我们认为PPI 同比
增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行的风险 ；欧美经济回落速度 偏快；国际局
势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 2

CPI 
9 月 CPI 环比增速较 8 月回落。9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，增速较 8 月下降 0.2 个百分点。从分项环
比看，9 月环比上行的包括交通通信 （1.0%） 、 衣着 （0.6%） 、 食品烟酒 （0.2%） 、 医疗保健 （0.2%），
环比增速下降的有生活用品及服务 （-0.1%） 、 教育文化娱乐 （-0.3%） 、 其他用品和服务 （-0.8%），
居住价格环比增速持平；9 月分项中较 8 月环比增速上升的有衣着、医疗保健、生活用品及服务，
较 8 月环比增速下降的有食品烟酒、教育文化娱乐、交通通信、其他用品和服务，居住环比增速持
平于 8 月。 
9 月 CPI 同比增速略低于万得一致预期。9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较 8 月上升 0.1 个百分点，核心
CPI 同比增长 1.0%，较8 月持平 ， 服务价格同比增长0.8%，较8 月持平 ， 消费品价格同比增长1.0%，
较 8 月上升 0.2 个百分点。从分项同比看，9 月同比增速增长的是其他用品和服务（5.9%） 、交通通
信（4.3%） 、医疗保健 （2.7%） 、教育文化娱乐 （1.4%） 、衣着 （1.2%） 、生活用品及服务 （0.2%），
同比增速下降的是居住（-0.3%）和食品烟酒（-0.9%）；同比增速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居
住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服
务增速均较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
9 月食品价格分化，牛羊肉价格环比涨幅扩大。从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上
月相同，低于季节性水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点；鲜菜、猪肉、鸡蛋和
鲜果价格涨幅在 0.5%-2.3%之间，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.05 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格持续上
涨，本月分别上涨 3.1%和 1.4%，涨幅均有扩大，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点；休渔期
结束，水产品供应充足，价格下降 0.8%，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.02 个百分点。从同比看，食品价格
同比下降 1.7%，降幅比上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点；食品中，猪肉
和鲜菜价格分别下降 10.0%和 8.1%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点；粮食、食用油、奶类
和鲜果价格降幅在 0.6%-1.5%之间 ， 合计影响CPI 同比下降约 0.07 个百分点 ； 鸡蛋价格上涨17.4%，
涨幅回落 1.1 个百分点 ， 影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ； 牛肉和羊肉价格分别上涨 7.5%和 6.4%，
涨幅继续扩大，合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点。 
服务价格和黄金价格环比回落 ，AI 产品价格同比涨幅扩大。从环比看 ，9 月服务价格由上月上涨 0.1%
转为下降 0.2%，对CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 服务中，
随着暑假结束，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格合计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.12 个百分点；秋季
开学，教育服务价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点；工业消费品价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点，其中，受国际市场价格变动影响，国内汽油价格和黄金饰品价格两项合计影响 CPI 环比
上涨约 0.14 个百分点；受成本走高影响，移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格合计
影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点 ； 秋装上市 ， 带动服装价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点。
从同比看，9 月汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点；扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ； 其中 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备、
平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点；服务价格涨幅与上月
相同，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点。 
AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，其中，食品价格影
响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点 ， 汽油价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点 ， 扣除能源的工业消
费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ； 从细项
看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上
涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨
约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备 、 平板电脑 、 计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.23
个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能
源价格和黄金价格的持续扰动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面， 9 月也出现了新的
特点 ， 最为明显的是AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8 月上升的重要因素 ， 体现
在通信工具价格 9 月同比增长 14.3%、交通通信项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代
表的食品价格，出现环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持相对平
稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不
确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。

### Page 4

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 4

PPI 
PPI 环比大幅走高。9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，较8 月上升 0.8 个百分点 ， 其中生产资料环比增长1.4%，
生活资料环比增长 0.1%。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较 8 月上升 1.2 个百分点，其中生产资料同比增
长 6.5%，较 8 月上升 1.5 个百分点，生活资料同比下降 0.1%，较 8 月上升 0.4 个百分点。 
9 月 PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9%，较 8 月上升 1.1 个百分点；从构成来看，9 月同比增速
上升较多的是有色金属材料类（ 20.0%），燃料、动力类（ 13.6%）和化工原料类（ 12.6%），同比
增速下降的仅有建筑材料类（-2.3%），农副产品类（-0.4%）和黑色金属材料类（-0.4%）；所有品
类中，同比增速较 8 月上升较多的是燃料、动力类，化工原料类和建筑材料类，同比增速下降的仅
有黑色金属材料类。 
9 月主要行业中出厂价格同比增速较高的是，煤炭开采和洗选业（ 35.3%），石油和天然气开采业
（24.5%）和石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业（21.6%），同比增速较低的是酒、饮料和精制茶制造业
（-5.3%），电力、热力的生产和供应业（-3.9%）和黑色金属矿采选业（-3.4%）；同比增速较 8 月
上升较多的是石油和天然气开采业，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业和煤炭开采和洗选业，下降的仅
有黑色金属矿采选业，造纸及纸制品业，电力、热力的生产和供应业以及木材加工及木、竹、藤、
棕、草制品业。 
输入性因素和季节性因素对 PPI 同比增速影响较大。9 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点 ：一是国际原油、
有色金属价格上行带动国内相关行业价格上涨 。 石油开采 、 精炼石油产品制造 、 有机化学原料制造、
有色金属矿采选业 、 有色金属冶炼和压延加工业 ，5 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.63 个百分点。
二是部分能源和原材料行业供需状况变化拉动价格上涨。受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、主产矿区供应
阶段性偏紧、国际煤价上涨预期效应等因素影响，煤炭开采和洗选业、煤炭加工价格环比上涨，钢
材、水泥等建筑材料需求有所回升，加之原材料成本上涨，非金属矿物制品业价格在环比连降 7 个
月后转为上涨，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比上涨，4 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.38
个百分点。三是人工智能、算电协同等领域加快发展带动部分行业 需求增加、价格上涨。电气机械
和器材制造业 、 计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业2 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.07 个百分点。
从同比看，价格上涨的行业中，煤炭开采和洗选业、石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加
工业、化学原料和化学制品制造业、有色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业、电气机械和
器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业，8 个行业涨幅比上月均扩大，合计影响 PPI 同比
上涨约 5.42 个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 1.12 个百分点，价格下降的行业中，酒饮料和精制茶制
造业、电力热力生产和供应业、医药制造 业、非金属矿物制品业、汽车制造业， 5 个行业合计下拉
PPI 同比约 0.66 个百分点。 
关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较
大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，从行业看主要 的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化
工等受输入性因素影响较大的行业 ，二是受AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业 ， 三是受季节性因素
影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具
体情况，都可能对 PPI 价格造成影响，但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化，我们认为
PPI 同比增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。

公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达 克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

## Document 116: 9月金融数据点评：好于预期的数据从哪来？

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月金融数据点评：好于预期的数据从哪来？.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
9 月份，新增社会融资规模 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192亿元；新增人民币贷款 1.08万亿元，同比少增 2100
亿元。
评论：
图1：金融数据分项一览
资料来源：Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
结论：9 月金融数据总体好于市场预期，开年后持续走弱的局面有所缓和。但结构上，政府债券支撑社融
季末回升，非政府部门融资需求仍然较弱。社融新增 3.55万亿元、同比多增 192亿元；信贷新增 1.08 万
亿元，但同比仍少增 2100 亿元。企业中长贷是贷款端主要支撑，居民端暂时停止缩表；M1回落至 3.2%、
与 M2的剪刀差重新走阔至 4.0 个百分点；财政资金投放加快，9 月财政缺口预计冲高。值得注意的是，新
型政策性金融工具开始落地，9月投放规模或许显著超过部分测算，达 3000亿左右。
展望后续，10 月低基数下金融数据同比读数有望延续改善。去年 10月新增贷款 2200 亿元、新增社融 8178
亿元，基数偏低。四季度新型政策性金融工具 9 月启动首批投放后将持续发力，叠加近期的购房贷款贴息

政策，对企业中长期贷款和居民按揭能构成一定支撑。但在实体融资需求实质性回暖之前，贷款余额与社
融存量同比的下行趋势将会持续。
具体看，9 月数据：

### Page 2

社融季末回升，主要靠政府债支撑。9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192 亿元；政府债券净融资 1.53
万亿元、同比多增 3438亿元，是同比转正的主要支撑；扣除政府债券的非政府融资同比少增 3246亿元，
显示实体融资需求仍然较弱。此外，当月新型政策性金融工具集中落地，交叉比对公开信息发现，其投放
规模或达 3000亿元左右（其中直接公布的约 1000亿元为民间投资），即非政府融资中亦含有政策驱动的
成分。
信贷季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表。9 月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿，高于市场预期，同比少增 2100
亿元，贷款余额增速降至 4.8%、续创新低；企业中长贷是主要支撑，居民贷款净增为零，结束了连续两个
月的缩表。
总体来看，信用扩张中枢下移的趋势尚未扭转。政府债券融资放量叠加财政存款下降，财政资金季末投放
加快；受去年同期基数效应影响，M1 增速回落至 3.2%、与 M2 的剪刀差重新走阔。10 月低基数下同比读数
有望改善，随着政策效果逐步显现，四季度信贷有望边际修复。

解读：
 社融：政府债为主要支撑，非政府融资仍弱
9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，较 8 月的 1.66 万亿元明显回升，同比多增 192 亿元，基本持平于过去五年同
期约 3.57万亿元的均值；月末社融存量同比增长 7.1%，较上月回落 0.1个百分点，继续创 2003年有数据
以来的新低。结构上，政府债券净融资 1.53万亿元，占新增社融的 43.1%，同比多增 3438亿元，贡献度
达 1791%，是社融同比转正的主要支撑。9 月是社融季节性回升月份，当月环比增加约 1.89万亿元，明显
高于过去五年同期平均的约 0.74万亿元。各科目对同比增量的贡献排序：政府债券（+3438亿元）、外币
贷款（+478亿元）、直接融资（-402亿元）、非标（-1102亿元）、人民币贷款（-3342 亿元）
1
。
图2：社融的季节性 图3：新增社融及结构
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
 信贷：季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表
信贷季末回升，高于市场预期。9月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿元，高于市场普遍预期的约 9900亿元，但

低于过去五年同期约 1.86 万亿元的均值，同比少增 2100亿元；9 月末贷款余额同比增长 4.8%，较上月回
落 0.1个百分点，继续创有数据以来新低。
居民端暂时停止缩表。9 月居民部门贷款净增为零，结束 7-8 月连续两个月的净减少：短期贷款减少 900
亿元、中长期贷款增加 874 亿元，分别同比少增 2321亿元和 1626亿元；9 月末落地的房贷贴息政策对按

## Document 178: Australia and NZ Weekly 5 October 2026 (2026-10-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia and NZ Weekly 5 October 2026 (2026-10-05).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 12

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIA &
NEW ZEALAND
WEEKLY
Week beginning 05 October 2026
In this week’s edition:
Economic Insight: RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected.
The Week That Was: Searching for signal.
Focus on New Zealand: Resilient activity and simmering inflation.

For the week ahead:
Australia: Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment, MI Inflation Gauge.
New Zealand: GlobalDairyTrade auction, NZIER Survey of Business Opinions, ANZ Commodity Prices.
Japan: Current account balance, household spending.
China: Foreign reserves, M2 money supply.
Euro Area: Sentix investor confidence, retail sales.
United States: FOMC Meeting Minutes, ISM Services PMI, University of Michigan Sentiment Index.
Global: S&P Global PMIs.
Analysis and forecasts for this week’s key releases.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

## Document 180: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

## Document 221: Daiwa_20261007_Acter 5536

Source PDF: all report pdf/Daiwa_20261007_Acter 5536.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 26
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

7 October 2026

Acter

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

### Page 6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides 
integrated cleanroom 
solutions via various 
subsidiaries

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.

By product, cleanroom electromechanical services remain Acter’s largest source of 
revenue (accounting for 66% of 1H26 revenue, per the company). These services include 
cleanroom architectural and MEP engineering, which powers airflow, filtration, 
environmental regulation and utility controls. Water, gas and chemical delivery systems are 
its second-largest source of revenue (accounting for 24% of 1H26 revenue, per the 
company). Contributed mainly by Novatech (6613 TT, not rated) and Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated), these services cover the main systems and hook-up systems, for  ultra-pure water 
(UPW), specialty gases, chemical piping, etc., all of which require highly precise build-up 
work. Except for cleanroom electromechanical and water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems, the rest of Acter’s revenue comes from the consumer industry services 
electromechanical (5%), biomedical (3%), and high-tech equipment and materials sales 
and services (1%).

Acter: revenue by product

Source: Company, Daiwa

Technically, Acter integrates digital transformation and green engineering into its business 
model, helping it differentiate from peers. Acter deploys digital twin technology and 1:1 3D 
BIM modelling to detect piping interferences and optimise spatial layout before installation, 
minimising schedule delays. This makes Acter a more reliable option, in our view, and thus 
being a favourable supplier, especially for the semiconductor clients that have extremely 
high quality and swiftness criterion.

Bright outlook: strong order visibility 
Rising orders on hand due to supply-demand imbalance for cleanrooms 
Acter's order backlog has increased from TWD55bn in 1Q26 to TWD60bn in 2Q26 and 
TWD66bn in July 2026. We expect this figure to further increase in the following quarters, 
given the supply-demand imbalance in the cleanroom sector. This is composed of 
semiconductor (63%), electronic assembly (11%), consumer (9%) and others (17%), per 
the company. In 2H25, the company accelerated the digestion of small-scale projects 
below TWD1bn, and, as a result, starting from 1H26, it successfully shifted its project

Back-end packaging, cloud infrastructure and hardware assembly are additional growth

qualified supplier status with US CSPs, providing mechanical, electrical and plumbing 
(MEP) integration, especially for those data centres that implement liquid-cooling systems. 
The company also wins factory construction projects for Taiwanese ODMs across the US 
and Southeast Asia, as well as PCB facility projects in Thailand and China. Non-tech 
sectors, including biotechnology, commercial buildings, smart logistics hubs and medical 
facilities, comprise 9% of the total backlog, per management.

Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

The supply of cleanroom 
services is limited due to 
high entry barriers in the 
segment

### Page 11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Acter: revenue growth trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.
